### Still unresolved

**M0246** — Reading: `\unsure{\circlearrowleft^{3}|\mathfrak S_3}`. The sign has an outer curved loop, inner curl and a small raised 3-like mark. The loop does not securely establish an arrowhead or the alternative script letter. Alternatives: \mathfrak S_3. Confidence: low.

**M0247** — Reading: `\unsure{\circlearrowleft|G}`. The sign has a rounded enclosing stroke and a short inward stroke; the latter does not securely distinguish the proposed G and circular-arrow readings. Alternatives: G. Confidence: low.

**M0248** — Reading: `\unsure{b}`. There is a dark upright/looped mark beside the left tetrahedral vertex, but the filled vertex and overlapping edge obscure the bowl needed to assert b. Confidence: low.

**M0249** — Reading: `\unsure{c}`. The short curved label is merged with dark adjoining vertex strokes; its opening cannot be isolated securely. Confidence: low.

**M0252** — Reading: `\unsure{0|\odot}`. The oval contains a dark central mark and short upright stroke; both a numerical label with overlap and a circled point remain possible. Alternatives: \odot. Confidence: low.

**M0256** — Reading: `\unsure{avec}`. The initial oval and following low joined strokes are partly crossed by the diagram. The final sequence is compatible with vec but not clear enough to remove doubt. Confidence: low.

**M0262** — Reading: `\unsure{17|14}`. The second digit combines a long descending 7-like stroke with an angular 4-like contour. The order in which they were drawn is not secure. Alternatives: 14. Confidence: low.

**M0263** — Reading: `\unsure{cartes|points|ponts}`. The descender-like first stroke, rounded next stroke, central tall stroke and low ending do not fully distinguish the alternatives. Alternatives: points|ponts. Confidence: low.

**M0264** — Reading: `\unsure{on|ou|or}`. The short word is present, with an initial loop and joined ending; the ending can be read as n, u or r. Alternatives: ou|or. Confidence: low.

**U24-QUATRE** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{quatre}}`. Heavy cancellation and inserted trois obscure part of the word. Confidence: low.

**U24-F** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{F}}`. Cancelled strokes do not securely establish F or an alternative letter. Confidence: low.

**U24-TYPE-PLACEMENT** — Reading: `\inserted{de type \((0,4)\)}`. Phrase is clear, but exact point of attachment in the linear text is not explicit. Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**Prefix before revêtements** — Reading: `\unsure{\(\deleted{\mathbf Z}n-\)|\(\deleted{\mathbf Z/n}\)}`. The overtraced Z-like figure, possible slash and cancellation extent cannot be separated securely. Alternatives: \(\deleted{\mathbf Z}n-\), \(\deleted{\mathbf Z/n}\). Confidence: low.

**Deletion status of revêtements** — Reading: `\unsure{revêtements|\deleted{revêtements}}`. The joined baseline and heavy final strokes can be retracing or cancellation; no distinct full-word strike is separable. Alternatives: revêtements, \deleted{revêtements}. Confidence: low.

**Word after ramifiés** — Reading: `\unsure{seulement}`. The medial letters are highly compressed and partly intersected by the large loop; every letter is not independently legible. Alternatives: seulement. Confidence: medium.

**First word of the struck phrase** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{variétés} algébriques d}`. The horizontal cancellation hides the low letter bodies in variétés; the word cannot be released from doubt letter by letter. Alternatives: variétés. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**Initial numeral of separate left count** — Reading: `2`. The numeral is overtraced and includes a lower loop and extra angled stroke. A prior numeral cannot be separated securely; no definite deleted digit has been invented. Alternatives: . Confidence: low.

**Beginning of lower struck line** — Reading: `\unsure{1\times4|\text{dont }4|4\times4}`. The cancellation crosses the lower strokes, so the upright and crossed angled form cannot be resolved into a certain word or product. Alternatives: \text{dont }4, 4\times4. Confidence: low.

**Faint underlying drawings** — Reading: `Pale contours retained in TikZ`. Coherent ovals, polygonal edges and vertex loops are visible, but their faintness limits the precision of individual contour junctions. Alternatives: . Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**I6 three group symbols** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak S|G}_J,\ \unsure{\mathfrak S|G}_3,\ \unsure{\mathfrak S|G}_3`. All three have a wavy upper stroke and broad lower loop; the alphabet is not settled by the ink. Alternatives: \mathfrak S, G. Confidence: low.

**upper compatibility symbol** — Reading: `\unsure{\vec Q|\vec G}`. The lower loop and right continuation do not securely separate Q from G. Alternatives: \vec Q, \vec G. Confidence: low.

**lower compatibility symbol** — Reading: `\unsure{\vec Q|\vec G}`. The retraced loop remains open to Q or G. Alternatives: \vec Q, \vec G. Confidence: low.

**fBs accent** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathrm{Bit\acute et}|\mathrm{Bitet}}`. The short upper stroke is faint in this occurrence; the lower key accent was adjudicated separately. Alternatives: Bitét, Bitet. Confidence: low.

**II2 struck word** — Reading: `\deleted{\text{\unsure{repères}}}`. The separate lower word is struck; not every letter can be distinguished from its crossing strokes. Alternatives: repères, unread canceled word. Confidence: low.

**II2prime struck word** — Reading: `\deleted{\text{\unsure{repères}}}`. The entire word after 4 sommets is crossed; its internal letters remain partly obscured. Alternatives: repères, unread canceled word. Confidence: low.

**II1 canceled group material** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{1}}`. The overwritten dark group entry cannot be read letter by letter. Alternatives: 1, unread canceled symbol. Confidence: low.

**II1 figure P labels** — Reading: `\unsure{P},\ \unsure{\bar P}`. The live P-like labels are heavily retraced; separate earlier letter forms cannot be securely recovered. Alternatives: P and bar P with unread overwritten strokes. Confidence: low.

**tau pi omega faint label** — Reading: `\unsure{\tau_{\pi,\omega}}`. The upper pencil label is faint and partly overwritten by the main annotation. Alternatives: \tau_{\pi,\omega}, unread tau subscript. Confidence: low.

**III2prime repère formula** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak X|\mathbb J}\unsure{(}I)`. The X-like letter and a possible left parenthesis cannot be separated confidently; one closing parenthesis is visible. Alternatives: \mathfrak X(I), \mathfrak X I), \mathbb J(I). Confidence: low.

**III2prime group blob** — Reading: `\unsure{\deleted{*}}`. The dark material after the quotient does not establish its original glyph or conclusively distinguish cancellation from retracing. Alternatives: canceled star-like mark, unread live or canceled strokes. Confidence: low.

**III2prime first figure overwritten labels** — Reading: `\unsure{\alpha'},\ \unsure{\beta},\ \unsure{\beta'}`. The top rear and lower two labels contain distinct overlapping dark strokes; the prior forms are not secure. Alternatives: alpha-prime, beta, beta-prime with unread underforms. Confidence: low.

**level0 middle index** — Reading: `\mathrm{III}_{\unsure{4|1}}`. On the reopened existing crop the small index has an angled head, cross-stroke and descending stem resembling the 4 in the same table. The repeated ink does not wholly exclude 1; 4 is retained first and no prime is added. Alternatives: 4, 1. Confidence: low.

**punctuation after Aut+** — Reading: `\unsure{;|,}`. A dot and a long curved descending stroke do not securely distinguish semicolon from comma. Alternatives: ;, ,. Confidence: low.

**parameter NB overwritten relation** — Reading: `\unsure{\simeq}`. The visible relation is tilde over a horizontal stroke, but separately crossed underlying strokes are unreadable. Alternatives: simeq with unread prior relation. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**1020 group, former subtotal** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{900}}`. Diagonal cancellation covers parts of the old digits; the 9-like loop and following round forms are not all plain. Alternatives: 900, other cancelled numeral. Confidence: low.

**1020 group, overwritten third label** — Reading: `\unsure{\tau'_r|\tau_r^1}`. The superposed upper-right strokes prevent a secure prime/exponent decision. Alternatives: \tau'_r, \tau_r^1. Confidence: low.

**1020 group, cancelled fifth-row equality** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{2\times60=2\times60}}`. Heavy blackening obscures the older factors below the clear replacement. The inherited reading remains doubtful. Alternatives: 2\times60=2\times60, another cancelled equality. Confidence: low.

**Total, cancelled former value** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{1580|1500}}`. Surviving counters favor 8 in the third position, but not every older digit is clear under the strike. Alternatives: 1580, 1500. Confidence: medium.

**Total, former multiplier** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{12}}`. Older curved head and base overlap the current two uprights; 12 is plausible but not securely separated. Alternatives: 12, another overwritten two-digit value. Confidence: low.

**Programme 1, article before syst.** — Reading: `\unsure{les|un}`. The tall initial favors l, but the compressed remainder does not completely exclude C’s un. Alternatives: les, un. Confidence: medium.

**Programme 1, cancelled word below orbites** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{paquets} de}`. Two descenders support p/q, but the intervening letters are crossed. The final de is separately visible. Alternatives: paquets de, another cancelled word followed by de. Confidence: low.

**Programme 1, acting group** — Reading: `\unsure{\mathfrak S_K|\mathfrak G_K}`. The large curved glyph permits S and G; no alphabetic decision is made. Alternatives: \mathfrak S_K, \mathfrak G_K. Confidence: medium.

**Programme 1, word before 542** — Reading: `\unsure{aux|avec}`. The short run favors aux, but its joins do not rule out avec. Alternatives: aux, avec. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled start between items 1 and 2** — Reading: `\deleted{2) \unsure{Réduire}}`. The initial capital and some tall strokes survive, but the crossing stroke hides the remainder; C’s proposed word is retained as a doubt. Alternatives: Réduire, another cancelled word beginning R. Confidence: low.

**Cancelled paragraph, initial short fragment** — Reading: `\unsure{qui} de type`. A q-like descender and a dot survive. The word before de type remains crossed and uncertain. Alternatives: qui, another short word. Confidence: low.

**Cancelled paragraph, verb after second qui** — Reading: `\unsure{reviennent|revient}`. Several low humps favor the plural, but their tops lie under the cancellation. Alternatives: reviennent, revient. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled paragraph, word after twist** — Reading: `\unsure{relationnel|relativement}`. The low middle arches favor relationnel; the ending is not plain enough to remove relativement. Alternatives: relationnel, relativement. Confidence: medium.

**Cancelled paragraph, beginning of last line** — Reading: `\unsure{un Autom.}`. The lower frame stroke passes through this word group, hiding parts of the abbreviated ending. Alternatives: un Autom., another cancelled abbreviation. Confidence: low.

**Programme 3, cancelled adjective** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{gauches}}`. Initial descending loop and a later tall stroke are compatible with gauches, but the horizontal strike obscures most small letters. Alternatives: gauches, another cancelled adjective. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**title, cancelled courbe** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{courbe}}`. The strike obscures the middle letters; beginning c, ascender b, and final e support but do not fully certify courbe. Alternatives: courbe. Confidence: low.

**title, characteristic phrase** — Reading: `\unsure{de car. \(0\)}`. The opening de is overtraced; the more legible remainder does not certify the whole phrase. Alternatives: de car. \(0\). Confidence: low.

**cancelled order-5 group, initial stroke** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\nu}\,g\) d’ordre \(5\)}`. An extra nu-like stroke precedes g under the cancellation; its identity is not secure. Alternatives: \nu, unidentified stroke. Confidence: medium.

**cancelled il est** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{il est}}`. The cancellation runs through the middle and terminal letters. The original doubt remains. Alternatives: il est. Confidence: low.

**case 2, cancelled initial letter** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{K|\Sigma}=\operatorname{card}I^g=1\)}`. The crossed and entangled initial cannot securely be separated into both K arms; a Sigma-like outline remains possible. No separate superscript g is visible before the equals sign. Alternatives: K, \Sigma. Confidence: medium.

**case 2, pole symbol** — Reading: `\(\unsure{\infty}\)`. Compact intersecting loops are overtraced, especially at the left. Alternatives: \infty. Confidence: medium.

**case 3, cancelled initial symbol** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\Sigma|K}\)}`. A Sigma-like angular outline survives, but the crossed strokes prevent an unqualified identification. Alternatives: \Sigma, K. Confidence: medium.

**association, cancelled symbol after Sigma_J** — Reading: `\(\Sigma_J\deleted{\unsure{\Sigma}}\)`. Several crossing loops obscure the small angular symbol after the live Sigma_J. Alternatives: \Sigma. Confidence: low.

**association, inclusion formula exponent** — Reading: `\(P_J\subset\Sigma_J^{\unsure{g}}\)`. The small loop and descending stroke on the right are not securely separable into exponent g and subscript J. Alternatives: \Sigma_J^g, \Sigma_J. Confidence: low.

**ending, field letter** — Reading: `\(\unsure{k|K}=\mathbb C\)`. An overtraced stem with joined right branches does not securely distinguish case. Alternatives: k, K. Confidence: medium.

**third margin, initial word** — Reading: `\unsure{Partition}`. The initial is p/P-like but the linked middle strokes do not certify the proposed word. Alternatives: Partition. Confidence: low.

**third margin, word before ou symétries** — Reading: `\unsure{cardinaux|valeurs}`. The raised mark, looped ascender, and low ending do not securely distinguish the two witness candidates. Alternatives: cardinaux, valeurs. Confidence: low.

### Still unresolved

**a: cancelled opening fragment** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{Le cas|un cas}}`. The long strike obscures some short joining strokes. The tall initial favours Le, but not every struck letter is independently clear. Alternatives: Le cas, un cas. Confidence: medium.

**left oblique margin: cancelled initial word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{pour}}`. The descending first stroke and remaining low run support pour, but the strike hides the internal joins. Alternatives: pour. Confidence: medium.

**c: tiny cancelled fragment after i.e.** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{?}}`. The dense retracing does not reveal the former character. Alternatives: ?, unidentified cancelled character. Confidence: low.

**c: first word of insertion** — Reading: `\unsure{Soit}`. The initial and interior letters have been heavily retraced; Soit remains the readable proposal rather than a secure resolution. Alternatives: Soit. Confidence: medium.

**c: middle of first cancelled line** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{comme repère}}`. The rising strike runs through the letter bodies. The ascenders and descenders admit the phrase but do not expose every letter. Alternatives: comme repère. Confidence: medium.

**c: inserted word over muni d’** — Reading: `\unsure{admettant}`. The raised word is cramped against the cancelled line and its internal joins remain uncertain. Alternatives: admettant. Confidence: medium.

**c: cancelled sign before involutif** — Reading: `\deleted{\(\unsure{\sigma}\)}`. A dense crossed loop remains; the original sigma cannot be isolated. Alternatives: \sigma, unidentified cancelled sign. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: live realizations label** — Reading: `\unsure{modulaires|spéciales}`. The low initial humps favour modulaires, but the rest overlaps cancelled writing. Alternatives: modulaires, spéciales. Confidence: medium.

**lower chart: cancelled word under realizations** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{polyédriques}}`. Heavy crossing strokes obscure the interior letters and the exact overlap with the upper label. Alternatives: polyédriques. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: mark following first 60** — Reading: `\unsure{(|c}`. A small isolated right-opening curve has no visible closing mate; whether it is punctuation or a letter is unsettled. Alternatives: (, c. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: cancelled caption** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{tétraèdre-pyr|tétraèdre-pyramide}}`. The right end is a short crossed run after a p descender; no full ending can be securely separated. Alternatives: tétraèdre-pyr, tétraèdre-pyramide, tétraédrales followed by unread text. Confidence: low.

**lower chart: first word of lower parenthesis** — Reading: `\unsure{tétraèdre|pentagonales}`. Retouching masks several joins. The taller stems favour tétraèdre, while the competing H reading is retained for blind review. Alternatives: tétraèdre, pentagonales. Confidence: medium.

**lower chart: isolated cancelled words at left** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{les types}}`. The cancellation is too dense for a complete letter-by-letter recovery. Alternatives: les types. Confidence: low.

**NB: earlier state under the last factor 2** — Reading: `2`. The present 2 is clear and heavily retraced. An earlier numeral cannot be separated from the thickened strokes; no former digit is supplied. Alternatives: retraced 2, 2 over an unidentified earlier digit. Confidence: medium.

**bottom sketch: central glyph** — Reading: `\unsure{\sigma}`. The open rounded form with an inward upper-right hook does not securely identify its alphabet or status. Alternatives: \sigma, 0, 3, curved orientation mark. Confidence: low.

**title, sign after holomorphe** — Reading: `\unsure{à|:}`. An upper angled mark and lower heavy loop/dot do not securely distinguish an accented a from a colon. Alternatives: à, :. Confidence: medium.

**sum membership target** — Reading: `\unsure{t_2|T_2}`. A strong crossbar excludes an ordinary L, but the protruding stem and heavy foot leave lowercase t versus capital T uncertain. Alternatives: t_2, T_2. Confidence: medium.

**live condition, sign in first parentheses** — Reading: `(\xi\unsure{\wedge|+}\eta)^2-4\xi\wedge\eta\ne0`. The small middle sign is peaked, unlike the clear plus in the preceding sum, but its reduced shape could include a crossing. The later product wedge is clear and is retained. Alternatives: \wedge, +. Confidence: medium.

**short cancellation after discriminant** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{un}}`. The strike obscures most of the low letters; un remains only a tentative recovery. Alternatives: un. Confidence: low.

**cancelled expansion after Notons** — Reading: `\deleted{$\unsure{\xi^2+\eta^2-2\xi\wedge\eta\ne}$}`. The raised shape after the first xi and several crossings are not independently plain. The eta-square, expanded layout and peaked wedge between the last factors support the retained tentative expression. Alternatives: \xi^2+\eta^2-2\xi\wedge\eta\ne. Confidence: low.

**abbreviated element** — Reading: `\unsure{élt}`. The word is visibly abbreviated, but the initial accent/letter is not secure enough to remove the existing doubt. Alternatives: élt, elt. Confidence: medium.

**final cancelled square** — Reading: `\deleted{$\unsure{(\xi+\eta)^2}$}`. The outer parentheses and raised 2 survive; the central xi-plus-eta strokes are partly hidden by the heavy cancellation. The formula gains doubt without a certainty-only changes row. Alternatives: (\xi+\eta)^2. Confidence: medium.

### Still unresolved

**first line: ambiguous mark before e1** — Reading: `\unsure{\deleted{\illegible{1}}|---}`. The short thick patch does not expose underlying letters. A cancelled unread fragment is favoured, but a heavy dash cannot be excluded. No former word or character count is asserted. Alternatives: \deleted{\illegible{1}}, ---. Confidence: medium.

**second line: cancelled terminal remnant of change** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{r}}`. A dense patch follows the readable change. A cancelled r would account for the old changer reading, but the letter itself is not fully recoverable. Alternatives: r, unidentified cancelled ending. Confidence: low.

**second line: separately cancelled short word** — Reading: `\deleted{\unsure{par}}`. The strike obscures the interior joins. The visible descending p and low rounded run admit par, without exposing every letter. Alternatives: par. Confidence: medium.
